## Story

**Added by Lionel, 2026-10-19 (release blocker):** "A: Fix, capitals only (Rec.)". His option: « Zorbal » also reaches « ZORBAL », never a word in small letters. The question he answered, word for word, after the PO's measurements (QA's finding on 10.15, PR #106, measured by the PO on 2026-10-19): "Capitals in 'every place' (10.15 command line, 10.17 desktop): a name you add by hand is replaced only where it is written with exactly the capitals you typed. Measured with the real detector: in « Monsieur Zorbal a présenté le budget. Brimel Testar a validé la proposition de ZORBAL. », adding « Zorbal » leaves « ZORBAL » in clear, in a text file and in Excel. In four invented test texts the detector found « Zorbal » at all 5 of its places but « ZORBAL » at only 2 of 8. When every « Zorbal » was already found, the command line even says « 'Zorbal' is already in the review as PERSON at every place: nothing added » while two « ZORBAL » stay in clear. Corrections have an older gap of the same kind: if the review groups « Zorbal » and « ZORBAL » as one name and you correct it to « Zorbal Quentrix », the place « ZORBAL QUENTRIX » is written « [pseudonym] QUENTRIX » (command line, already in v2.2.0; desktop single-document review). (A) Fix before 2.3.0, forms with capitals only (Rec.): « Zorbal » also reaches « ZORBAL », never a word in small letters; none of the app's 728 known names appears in capitals in your ebook (20,000 words) or in the 25 test documents, so this adds no wrong replacement there. (B) Fix before 2.3.0, capitals ignored entirely: it also reaches small-letter words, so adding the surname « Petit » would also replace « petit », 8 times in your ebook. (C) Later (backlog). For A or B: 10.17's rule changes before its development starts, and the command line and the corrections get one small new story after 10.15 merges. Which one?" The options: "A: Fix, capitals only (Rec.)" / "B: Fix, ignore capitals" / "C: Later (backlog)". As the question says, 10.17's rule changed before its development started (Story 10.17, AC1); this story is the "one small new story after 10.15 merges" for the command line and the corrections. Lionel's answer to its Q1 (2026-10-19, "B: Erasure only, in 10.19 (Rec.)") adds the erasure across capitals (AC4), and the rule takes the PO's wording, accepted by the orchestrator without a question (Decisions Recorded). On 2026-10-19 Lionel also approved the `delete-mapping` texts (Q2, AC8), extended the rule to capitals without accents (Q3, "A: Reach it too (Rec.)", AC1, AC4, AC5; the desktop, 10.17, takes the same rule), and extended corrections in desktop batch review to the rows written with other capitals (Q4, "A: Reach it too (Rec.)", AC3), which the PM placed in this story (Ordering).

**As a** user who adds a name by hand or corrects a name in the review,
**I want** the name also replaced where it is written in capitals, and an erasure by name to remove every row whose name differs from it only in capitals,
**so that** « ZORBAL » does not stay in clear when I added or corrected « Zorbal », and erasing « Zorbal » leaves no « ZORBAL » row.

**Priority:** HIGH — release blocker for v2.3.0 (Lionel, 2026-10-19). A leak: a name the user added or corrected stays in clear where it is written in capitals.
**Change type:** the places of a name added with [A] in the CLI (10.15's `places_of` and `is_listed_place`), and the shared function that locates a correction (`ValidationWorkflow._locate_modified_span`), used by the CLI's [E] and by the desktop's single-document review; the reach of a correction in desktop batch review (`gui/models/validation_state.py`, `_action_rows`, Q4); `delete-mapping` by name (Q1, Q3) and its screen line and reference paragraph (Q2). No detection change, so G1, G3 and G5 are N/A. No file under `gdpr_pseudonymizer/nlp/`, `gdpr_pseudonymizer/resources/`, `tests/accuracy/` or `tests/test_corpus/` changes.
**Ordering:** outside the detector chain. It starts after 10.15, merged as `90a1646` (PR #106), because it changes 10.15's `places_of` and `is_listed_place`. It merges after 10.17 and is rebased on it, because Q4 changes `_action_rows` in `gui/models/validation_state.py`, which 10.17 also changes (10.16 changes that file too, in other functions). Q4 is part of this story rather than a story of its own (the PM's choice, 2026-10-19): it is the batch-review half of this story's correction fix (b), it uses the same `is_form` rule, and one QA gate and one Codex review then cover the whole correction rule; the cost is this later merge. It shares the correction locating with 10.17: `_locate_modified_span` is called from `gui/models/validation_state.py` (`:378`, `:443`, `:800`) and `gui/screens/validation.py` (`:702`), and 10.17 changes `validation_state.py`. 10.16 also changes `validation_state.py`, but not the functions that call `_locate_modified_span`. 10.17 applies the same capitals rule to the desktop's places: whichever of 10.17 and 10.19 merges second reuses the first one's code for it, so the two apps keep one rule. It shares `validation/models.py` with 10.13 and 10.15, `validation/workflow.py` with 10.13, 10.15 and 10.18, and the `delete-mapping` code (`cli/commands/delete_mapping.py`, `data/repositories/mapping_repository.py`) with 10.12, now merged. Its own PR; stories that change the same file are sequenced and the later one is rebased on the earlier, never merged together. Every story edits `CHANGELOG.md`. Before the v2.3.0 release.

**Branch:** `story/10.19-capitals-forms`, rebased by the SM on `main` `097a12c` (10.20 and 10.17 merged) on 2026-10-25 and pushed with a lease on `1244103`. The PM's epic commits are now `3fe0d59`, `273b987` and `318def4` (before: `0706e51`, `0b8bd90` and `7ab459e`; first rebased on `90a1646` on 2026-10-19 from `af52dbe` and `5450ef3`); the SM's v1.0 to v1.4 are now `5cc01bd`, `09a6523`, `d646814`, `df74a53`, `d1162a2` and `b7afc29` (before: `6474726`, `f875def`, `d1b0469`, `a68410e`, `a69d187` and `1244103`); v1.5 follows. The rebase met three conflicts, all in the epic, each between 10.19's lines and the lines 10.18 and 10.20 added (the stories table, 10.19's section beside 10.20's, the risks table): every line of `main` was kept and 10.19's lines added as they were (`rgpd-analysis/r1019/sm/rebase/resolve3.py`, a line-level three-way merge). The story file came through unchanged. This story's commits change `docs/stories/` only. Pushed to `origin` since v1.2 (the orchestrator's instructions); no PR yet (the dev opens it as a draft, Task 1.6).

**Code authorship:** the BMAD dev agent (Claude) writes the tests first, then the fix (AC9; Lionel, 2026-10-18: Codex reviews, never writes). Before the merge command, the orchestrator runs one read-only `codex exec` review of the PR's final head, and runs it again only if the code changes after it. If Codex cannot run, the work stops and Lionel is told. The SM's prototype, sketch and measurement files are Claude-written measurement files: never committed, never applied in the worktree (Dev Notes "Environment").

### Context

Copied word for word from the epic at `318def4` (the rebased `7ab459e`, the same text):

At `main` `e35ccc5`, and on 10.15's PR #106 (head `2143813`, QA gate at `cc3c79c`). Measured by the PO on 2026-10-19 (notes `rgpd-analysis/r1015/po/po_caps_1015_1017_notes.md`; probes in `r1015/po/caps/` and `r1017/po/probe/test_po_caps_desktop.py`; invented names only).

- **The real detector misses most capital forms.** On four invented French texts it found the title form « Zorbal » at all 5 of its places, and the capital form « ZORBAL » at only 2 of 8 (for example « Présents : Vorlan ZORBAL, … la proposition de ZORBAL. »: both missed).
- **(a) [A] reaches only the exact text typed.** 10.15's `places_of` (`validation/models.py:274`, PR #106) matches the exact text, so adding « Zorbal » with [A] leaves « ZORBAL » in clear, on ".txt" and ".xlsx":
  - « Présents : Vorlan ZORBAL, … Monsieur <pseudonym> a présenté le budget. … la proposition de ZORBAL. »: "ZORBAL" twice in clear;
  - when every « Zorbal » was already found, the CLI shows the approved 10.15 line « ℹ️ 'Zorbal' is already in the review as PERSON at every place: nothing added » (`validation/workflow.py:42`, PR #106) while two « ZORBAL » stay in clear. `is_listed_place` (`validation/models.py:306`, PR #106) compares the exact text, so a capital place found by the detector is not recognised as found either.
  - v2.2.0 is the same or worse: it replaced one place only, with the exact capitals (`str.find`).
- **(b) An older correction leak across capitals.** The review groups « Zorbal » and « ZORBAL » as one name, because `_is_person_variant` compares person names in small letters (`nlp/entity_grouping.py:133`, `:139-140`). A correction then looks for the new text with its exact capitals (`ValidationWorkflow._locate_modified_span`, `validation/workflow.py:550-578`, `str.find` at `:571`, `:575`); at a capital place it does not find it and keeps the old span. Measured, detection replaced, « Zorbal Quentrix a signé. Copie : ZORBAL QUENTRIX. », one group corrected to « Zorbal Quentrix »:
  - CLI on PR #106: « Maria Gonçalves a signé. Copie : Maria Gonçalves QUENTRIX. »; v2.2.0: the same kind of output;
  - desktop single-document review, at `1f35dae` and on the 10.17 prototype: « Julien Silva a signé. Copie : Julien Silva QUENTRIX. »: the desktop calls the same function (`gui/models/validation_state.py:378`, `:443`, `:800`; `gui/screens/validation.py:702`);
  - desktop batch: each place is its own row, and a correction reaches the rows with the same name key (10.10, `_action_rows`, `gui/models/validation_state.py:315`; `_name_key` `:302`), which keeps capitals. Measured by the PO (Q4): « Zorbal » found in « Zorbal Quentrix a signé. » and « ZORBAL » in « Signé : ZORBAL QUENTRIX. »; the « Zorbal » row corrected to « Zorbal Quentrix »: the « ZORBAL » row does not take it and stays to review; accepted as it is, the file reads « … Signé : [pseudonym] QUENTRIX. », today and with this story's fix before Q4.

  "QUENTRIX" stays in clear. So 10.9's and 10.10's "every place" corrections reach a grouped capital place but write over its old span.
- **(c) Two pseudonyms for one person, and a partial erasure (measured by the PO with the real CLI, PR #106 and v2.2.0 the same).** A stored name is keyed by its exact text (`core/mapping_key.py:17-35` keeps capitals; `_resolve_pseudonyms` gives one pseudonym per key and type, `core/document_processor.py:255`, `:712`).
  - With the real detector, « Entretien avec Quentrel Zorbal. Quentrel ZORBAL explique son parcours. … »: the review shows one name, accepted once, but two rows are stored, ('Quentrel ZORBAL' → 'Évangéline Caron') and ('Quentrel Zorbal' → 'Évangéline Hamel'), and the output reads « Entretien avec Évangéline Hamel. Évangéline Caron explique son parcours. … ». The same happens when the detector found « Zorbal » and « ZORBAL » is added by hand.
  - Erasure: `delete-mapping Zorbal --force` deletes only the ('Zorbal') row and prints « The pseudonym 'Christian Henriques' is now permanently anonymous. », while ('ZORBAL' → 'Astrid Daniel') stays. `delete-mapping` finds and deletes rows by the exact name (`cli/commands/delete_mapping.py:129`, `:159`; `find_all_by_full_name`, `data/repositories/mapping_repository.py:205`, `_query_by_full_name` `:216`, `delete_entities_by_full_name` `:443`), an equality on the encrypted name. The search step of the documented erasure workflow (`docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md:633-638`), `list-entities --search Zorbal`, lists both rows, since its search ignores capitals.
  - Nothing stays in clear in the written files.
- **Lionel's rule and the text it was weighed on:** the app's name list (`resources/french_names.json`, 728 names) holds ordinary French words. In Lionel's ebook (20,157 words), 10 of those names appear as small-letter words 22 times (« petit » ×8), but none appears in all capitals; the 25 main-corpus documents write no name in capitals. So forms with capitals add no wrong replacement on the text measured, while ignoring capitals entirely would (adding « Petit » would replace « petit »).
- **Why the rule reads "the same letters, whatever their capitals" (the PO's M1):** the PM's first wording listed three forms (as typed, each word's first letter in capitals, all capitals). The PO measured that it misses « Vorlan ZORBAL » for « Vorlan Zorbal », « Marie de Zorbal » for « MARIE DE ZORBAL », « O'Zorbal », « Jean-Zorbal » and « LeZorbal », and that the new wording adds 0 places beyond the exact name on the real text (the app's 728 names, Lionel's ebook, the 25 main-corpus documents).
- **Accents dropped in capitals (measured by the PO, Q3):** with this story's fix and Lionel's first rule, adding « Hélène » writes « HÉLÈNE » with its pseudonym and leaves « HELENE » in clear; v2.2.0 and today's code leave both in clear; `delete-mapping Hélène` leaves the saved pseudonym of « HELENE ». Reaching it adds no wrong replacement on the text measured: none of the app's 728 names appears in capitals, with or without accents, in Lionel's ebook or in the 25 main-corpus documents.
- **`delete-mapping --force` (the PO's S1):** with `--force` no confirmation screen is shown (`cli/commands/delete_mapping.py:143-145`); the deleted names are named only in the success lines (`:200-216`). The screen today says « You are about to delete the following N entity mappings (one per type of this name): » (`_display_confirmation`, `:293-312`), and the reference says "one row each" (`docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md:470`, `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.fr.md:470`).
- **Not measured:** how often real documents write names in capitals, or drop accents in capitals.

## Acceptance Criteria

AC1 to AC10 are copied from the epic's Story 10.19 at `318def4` (the rebased `7ab459e`, the same text) word for word. "AC precisions (story-level)" adds detail and does not change the epic text. AC11 to AC15 are story-level.

1. **AC1 — The capitals rule (Lionel, 2026-10-19):** a form of a text is any whole-word occurrence of the same letters as the text selected, whatever their capitals, except a place written entirely in small letters (unless it is exactly the text selected). In the CLI, "the text selected" is the text typed with [A] or [E]. Lionel's rule: « Zorbal » also reaches « ZORBAL », never a word in small letters. **Accents in capitals (Lionel's Q3):** a place written entirely in capitals is also a form when its letters are the name's letters once accents are set aside (« HELENE », « HELÈNE » for « Hélène »); still never a word in small letters, and « Helene » (no accent, not in capitals) stays a different name. So « ZORBAL », « Vorlan ZORBAL » for « Vorlan Zorbal », « Marie de Zorbal » for « MARIE DE ZORBAL », « ZorBAL », « HÉLÈNE » and « HELENE » for « Hélène » are forms, and « petit » is never a form of « Petit ». Words are whole words as in 10.15 (a hyphen joins words). One rule serves the CLI here and the desktop in 10.17.
2. **AC2 — [A] reaches every form (a):** in the CLI, a name added with [A] is replaced at every form of it (AC1), in a text document and in every cell of a spreadsheet. Each place carries the text as added, so every form takes the added name's pseudonym and one row is stored. A place the detector found with the same span, type and cell, whatever the capitals of the text it found, is "already in the review" (`is_listed_place`) and keeps its own decision. So the approved line « … at every place: nothing added » appears only when no form is left unfound, which makes it true again.
3. **AC3 — A correction is found in its forms (b):** `_locate_modified_span` looks for the corrected text in any of its forms (AC1) around each place, so a grouped capital place (« ZORBAL QUENTRIX ») is covered whole by the correction (« Zorbal Quentrix »), and no part of it stays in clear. This holds for the CLI's [E] and for the desktop's single-document review, which call the same function. The corrected name keeps one pseudonym and one row. Where no form is found, the place keeps its span, as today. **In desktop batch review (Lionel's Q4),** where each place is its own row, a correction also reaches the rows of the same name and type written with other capitals, and without accents in capitals (AC1), except a row the user rejected on its own, and never a row written entirely in small letters: `_action_rows` matches `_name_key` by `is_form`, same type. This lifts the story's rule that desktop batch rows do not change.
4. **AC4 — Erasure across capitals (c), Lionel's Q1 and Q3:** `delete-mapping` with a name also removes the rows whose name differs from it only in capitals, and the rows written entirely in capitals whose letters are the name's once accents are set aside (`delete-mapping Hélène` also deletes the saved pseudonym of « HELENE »), listed with the others before the user confirms. With `--force`, no screen is shown and the rows are named only in the success lines, as today (the PO's S1). Each removed row gets its own erasure entry, as 10.12 writes them (no name). One pseudonym per spelling stays for a later story (Follow-ups).
5. **AC5 — Accents in capitals (Lionel's Q3):** « HELENE » and « HELÈNE » are forms of « Hélène » (AC1) for [A], for corrections and for `delete-mapping`; « Helene » is not. The desktop (10.17) takes the same rule.
6. **AC6 — Tests first, invented names only:** the BMAD dev agent writes the tests and commits them before the fix; they drive the real CLI with keys on ".txt" and ".xlsx", and the real desktop single-document and batch paths for AC3, detection replaced. Each expected failure is an `assert` on the written file, the stored rows or the line shown, made after the test's preconditions hold, and each failing test is marked `xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)` on the old code (the 10.5 pattern). The fix removes the markers. Cases:
   - [A] « Zorbal » on a text with « ZORBAL » at 2 places: both written with the added name's pseudonym, one row ('Zorbal', PERSON);
   - every « Zorbal » found, « ZORBAL » not: the approved "nothing added" line is not shown, and the capital places are added;
   - a capital place the detector found: it keeps its own decision;
   - [A] « Petit » on a text with « petit » and « PETIT »: « petit » never written, « PETIT » written;
   - a mixed form: [A] « Vorlan Zorbal » on a text with « Vorlan ZORBAL »: written with the added name's pseudonym;
   - a small particle: [A] « MARIE DE ZORBAL » on a text with « Marie de Zorbal »: written;
   - « SAINT-ZORBAL » for « Zorbal »: not a place (the hyphen joins);
   - the correction case of Context (b), CLI and desktop single document: no part of « ZORBAL QUENTRIX » in clear, one row;
   - **erasure (AC4):** rows ('Zorbal', PERSON) and ('ZORBAL', PERSON) stored; `delete-mapping Zorbal`: both are listed before the confirmation, both are deleted, each with its erasure entry; a row whose letters differ (« Zorbalix ») stays;
   - **accents (Q3):** [A] « Hélène » on a text with « HÉLÈNE », « HELENE » and « Helene »: the first two written with the added name's pseudonym, « Helene » not; `delete-mapping Hélène` with saved pseudonyms of « Hélène » and « HELENE »: both listed and deleted;
   - **desktop batch (Q4):** the Context (b) case: correcting the « Zorbal » row to « Zorbal Quentrix » also corrects the « ZORBAL » row, and no part of « ZORBAL QUENTRIX » is in clear; a « ZORBAL » row rejected on its own stays rejected; a row written « zorbal » is not reached;
   - **the screen (Q2):** with two saved pseudonyms of the name, the confirmation screen shows the approved line with the count; with `--force`, the success lines name every deleted saved pseudonym.

   Existing tests stay green; a 10.15 test changes only if it encodes the exact-capitals rule, each for a stated reason.
7. **AC7 — CHANGELOG:** `[Unreleased]` gets a "Fixed" entry: a name added with [A] or corrected in the review is now also replaced where it is written in capitals (« ZORBAL » for « Zorbal »), never in a word in small letters, and in capitals without accents (« HELENE » for « Hélène »); in desktop batch review a correction also reaches the same name written with other capitals; the correction case was already in v2.2.0. And `delete-mapping` with a name now also deletes the saved pseudonyms of the same name written with other capitals or without accents in capitals, which earlier versions kept. The entry says "saved pseudonyms", never "rows" (Lionel's Q2 remark). The 10.15 entry's wording is aligned if it says "the exact text".
8. **AC8 — User copy (Lionel's Q2, approved word for word; the outer « » are quote marks):**
   - **The `delete-mapping` screen, for several saved pseudonyms** (the number is the count), replacing « You are about to delete the following N entity mappings (one per type of this name): »: « You are about to delete the following 2 saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type or its capitals): ». The one-row line is unchanged.
   - **`docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md`, replacing the paragraph at `:470`:** « The same name can have several saved pseudonyms: one for each type it was saved under, and one for each way it was written with capitals ("Marie Dupont", "MARIE DUPONT"). Deleting by name lists all of them, then deletes them all (each deletion is logged as an ERASURE entry); `--id` deletes only one and tells you which others remain for the same name written exactly the same way. »
   - **`docs/CLI-REFERENCE.fr.md`, replacing the paragraph at `:470`:** « Un même nom peut avoir plusieurs pseudonymes enregistrés : un pour chaque type sous lequel il a été enregistré, et un pour chaque façon dont il a été écrit avec des majuscules (« Marie Dupont », « MARIE DUPONT »). La suppression par nom les affiche tous, puis les supprime tous (chaque suppression est consignée par une entrée ERASURE) ; `--id` n'en supprime qu'un et indique ceux qui restent pour le même nom écrit exactement de la même façon. »
   - Any other new visible text is user copy that Lionel approves, word for word, before the story is done. User texts say "saved pseudonym", never "row", "ligne" or "mapping" (Lionel's Q2 remark).
9. **AC9 — Codex:** Lionel, 2026-10-18: "codex is NOT writing code, ever. Codex is a reviewer."; "codex is the reviewer for bmad projects, period, and don't substitute with forge without telling me first"; "forge cato and co are part of this. which is good, pai should keep and using it BUT NOT WITH BMAD". The BMAD dev agent (Claude) writes the code and the tests, tests first; no Forge, Anvil or Cato on this story. Before the merge command, the PR's final head gets one read-only Codex review (`codex exec --sandbox read-only`, run by the orchestrator, one session); if the code changes after that review, the new head is reviewed again. If Codex cannot run, the work stops and Lionel is told; no review is reported that did not run.
10. **AC10 — Gates:**
    - **G1, G3 and G5 are N/A:** there is no detection change. The PR states this explicitly. If any file the accuracy suite loads is touched, one regular `accuracy.yaml` run on the branch shows the 16 lines identical to `38212303870`, cited by ID.
    - **G4:** never in the same PR as a detector change, or as another Epic 10 story.
    - **G2:** QA re-runs the AC6 tests and the PO's capitals probes, checks out the tests-first commit to see the tests fail on the old code, and reads the diff. QA's gate comes before the merge.
    - **G6** applies in full (black, ruff, mypy, unit tests, full CI green).
    - **G7:** no headline number changes. CHANGELOG as above.
11. **AC11 — Tests first, proven on CI (story-level).** Before any product code changes, the tests of Dev Notes "Test Plan" are committed, written by the BMAD dev agent (AC6, AC9). Every test that fails on the unfixed code is marked `xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)`; its preconditions are `pytest.fail("PRE: …")` calls (never `assert`), so a broken precondition is a red failure even under that marker, and its expected failure is the first `assert` after them. The PR's CI on that commit is green only if each of them really fails, with an assertion, on every OS leg. The fix commit removes the markers and changes no assertion of the new tests.
12. **AC12 — What must not change (story-level).** A name typed or corrected with its exact capitals is placed and written as today (10.15); a correction whose exact text is found keeps today's span; a word written entirely in small letters is never a form (AC1); `delete-mapping` of a name with no other spelling deletes the same rows as today, with the same one-row screen (with several types, the several-row line is Lionel's new one, AC8); `delete-mapping --id` (except one word of its remaining line, AC15), `list-entities --search`, the known-name lookup (`find_by_full_name_and_type`), the mapping keys (one pseudonym per spelling, Follow-ups (a)), and the desktop review's accept and reject (`_group_ids`) and custom pseudonyms (`_pseudonym_rows`) do not change. No detection, schema or file-format change.
13. **AC13 — Consumer trace (story-level).** Dev Notes "Consumer Trace" lists every path of the three parts, today and after the fix: the CLI [A] forms and the approved line; the correction locating in the CLI, the desktop single-document review and the desktop batch review; the CLI review's groups; `delete-mapping` by name and the `--id` line. The dev re-checks every row at the fix commit and records any difference.
14. **AC14 — Isolation and invented strings (story-level; the standing rules).** No agent working on this story opens, lists, reads or greps anything under `tests/test_corpus/held_out/` or `tests/test_corpus/final_exam/`. The new test files sit in `tests/unit/`, which both leakage guards scan, so every string in them is an invented name (Zorbal, Zorbal Quentrix, Vorlan Mestrix, Vorlan Zorbal, Zorbalix, Vorlène, Vorlène Quentrix, Vorlene, Vorlene Quentrix, Quovôme Zorbal and their capital forms), a main-corpus word pre-checked by the SM (Test Plan), or a code identifier. See Dev Notes "Isolation Rules".
15. **AC15 — The `--id` line (Lionel's Q5b, story-level).** After `delete-mapping --id`, when other saved pseudonyms of the same name written exactly the same way remain, the two lines shown are today's with "row"/"rows" replaced by "mapping"/"mappings", and nothing else in them changed: with two left, « 2 other mappings with the same name remain (types: LOCATION, ORG). Delete one with 'gdpr-pseudo delete-mapping --id <uuid>', or every mapping of the name with 'gdpr-pseudo delete-mapping "<name>"'. »; with one left, « 1 other mapping with the same name remains (type: ORG). Delete one with 'gdpr-pseudo delete-mapping --id <uuid>', or every mapping of the name with 'gdpr-pseudo delete-mapping "<name>"'. ». T18 checks both, byte for byte once whitespace is collapsed (as T11 for Lionel's Q2 line). With none left, nothing more is shown, as today.

### AC precisions (story-level)

- **AC1, the rule:** a form of a text is a whole-word occurrence of the same letters as the text selected, whatever their capitals, except a place written entirely in small letters (unless it is exactly the text selected) (the PO's wording, accepted by the orchestrator; Decisions Recorded). "Whole word" is 10.15's: neither the character before nor the one after is a letter, a digit, an underscore or a hyphen (`-`, U+2010, U+2011), nor (since 10.20) a combining mark. "The same letters" means equal once both are put in small letters (`str.lower()`): « Zorbal », « ZORBAL », « ZorBAL » and « zorbal » have the same letters. "Entirely in small letters" means the place holds no capital letter: « zorbal » and « petit » are never forms of « Zorbal » and « Petit », while « Vorlan zorbal » is a form of « Vorlan Zorbal » (it holds a capital). Typed in small letters, « petit » reaches « petit » (exactly the text) and « Petit », « PETIT ». **Accents in capitals (Lionel's Q3):** a place written entirely in capitals (no small letter in it, and a capital letter: 10.17's `is_form`) is also a form when its letters, accents set aside, are the text's: « HELENE » and « HELÈNE » for « Hélène »; « Helene » (not in capitals, no accent) is not, nor « helene ». "Accents set aside" is 10.17's `fold` (the PO's S1): each character on its own, Unicode NFD without the combining marks, a character whose result would not be one character kept as it is. The length is kept, so a place's span is the same in the text and in its accent-free copy. Every text compared is composed (10.20's readers and typed text), so a document whose accents are stored as separate marks gives the same forms (T19). 10.17's functions, moved to `validation/models.py`, hold the rule for both apps (Fix Design, AC1; Ordering and Rebase).
- **AC2, "already in the review":** a place the detector found keeps its own review when it has the same span, type and cell and its text is a form of the typed text (`is_listed_place`; a found « ZORBAL » for an added « Zorbal », a found « HELENE » for an added « Hélène »); a place added by an earlier [A] of the same letters and type is not added twice. Every place takes the text as typed, so every form is written with the typed name's pseudonym and one row is stored for the typed spelling. The forms are typed places (`ADDED_BY_HAND_SOURCE`), so 10.15's widening (its AC14 (d), and the "10.15 Codex P0" ruling) applies to them as to any place added by hand. The approved line is unchanged; it shows only when no form is new (T2). v1.15 (ADD-1019-01; the orchestrator's ruling 6): a place an earlier [A] holds is recognised in either Q3 direction, or through the text written there: after [A] « Hélène », [A] « HELENE » adds nothing and shows the line; after [A] « Belora », which holds « BÊLORA », [A] « Bélora » adds « Bélora » only (T36 ×4, CLI and desktop).

Q3 and Q4 reach from the name to its capitals forms, not back: « ZELVINE TESTAR » does not reach « Zélvine Testar » (the orchestrator's CAPS-001 ruling). v1.10 (QA's PERF-001): the forms are searched in that window only. So the exact case is unchanged (AC12), and « ZORBAL » corrected as « Zorbal Quentrix » becomes « ZORBAL QUENTRIX », whole. CLI [E] (`workflow.py:346`) and the desktop single-document review (`gui/models/validation_state.py:524`, `:591`, `:1042`; `gui/screens/validation.py:730`) call the same function. **Small letters (the PO's N3), as Lionel's rule says:** a place of a corrected group written entirely in small letters (« zorbal quentrix ») is not a form, so it keeps its old span, as today: « zorbal » is replaced, « quentrix » stays.

With Q4, another row is reached when its type is the same and its name is a form of the corrected row's name (`is_form`: other capitals, or capitals without accents; T14, T16), except a row the user rejected on its own (`_decided_alone`, `:412-416`, as today); a row written entirely in small letters is never a form, so never reached. `_action_rows` also serves a type change in batch (10.10 ties them), so a type change reaches the same rows, as in the CLI and the single-document review, where the review already groups « Zorbal » with « ZORBAL » (`_is_person_variant`). Accept, reject (`_group_ids`) and custom pseudonyms (`_pseudonym_rows`) do not change. **Single-document review (the PO's S2):** « Vorlène » and « VORLENE » are not variants, so they are two rows there too, and the same `_action_rows` change reaches the second (T15). The PO's mutant mAA (`_action_rows` without the accents branch) fails T15 and T16 (measured). **A row that stands for several places (the orchestrator's ruling on the PO's v1.2 M1, Decisions Recorded):** in single-document review a row stands for several places (10.17's `_occurrences`: a detected row its variant group, a row added by hand the places 10.17 gave it), and another row reached through the forms rule (its `_name_key` is not exactly the corrected row's) is reached only when the text written at every one of its places (the document's text at that span; a place added by hand carries the name as typed, not that text) is a form of the corrected name (v1.8, T21). A row with exactly the same name and type is reached as today (10.10, Lionel's Q3 there; the orchestrator's ruling on the PO's v1.3 M1), whatever places it stands for: « Zorbal » added by hand and corrected reaches the detected « Zorbal » row that also stands for « zorbal » (G9). With « Vorlène », « VORLENE » and « Vorlene » in one document, the « VORLENE » row stands for « VORLENE » and « Vorlene », so it is not reached and keeps its own decision, as today (G8). Batch rows have one place each, so batch is unchanged. **v1.10 (QA's CAPS-001; the orchestrator's ruling):** correcting the « ZELVINE » row does not reach the « Zélvine » row, in single-document and in batch review; nothing is written silently there, because each row is decided on its own: the « Zélvine » row stays in the review, pending (G10). **v1.13 (the second Codex review's first two P0; the orchestrator's ruling):** a later correction of a row reaches every row its earlier correction reached (a row holding that corrected text), through the text now written at every place of that row, the span the earlier correction gave it: « Vorlène » corrected to « Vorlène Quentrix », then to « Vorlène Quentrix Zorbal », reaches the « VORLENE » row again through « VORLENE QUENTRIX » (T26, single document and batch). v1.15 (CAPS-004; the orchestrator's ruling 3): from either row: the shared earlier correction is read before the original names, so the « VORLENE » row extended to « Vorlène Quentrix Zorbal » reaches the « Vorlène » row too, which takes it only where the new text reaches every place it stands for (T32, single and batch). A row reached through the forms rule only takes a correction that reaches it, at every place it stands for, from the span written there and in Q3's direction (the corrected text, or a capitals form of it); otherwise it stays as it is, pending as on `main`, never written with a partial span: in batch, « ZÉLVINE » corrected to « ZELVINE TESTAR » leaves the « Zélvine » row pending (T27); in single-document review, a « ZELVINE » row standing for « ZELVINE MÉZOR » and « ZELVINE TESTAR » stays pending when « Zélvine » is corrected to « Zélvine Mézor » (T29). The row corrected and the rows of exactly the same name are corrected as today, except one point (v1.13, Lionel's answer, Decisions Recorded): where the correction is not found at one of their places, the words it adds are found right after the old span, in any capitals or accents (not a word in small letters only), and the span covers them: « ZÉLVINE » and « Zélvine » corrected as « ZELVINE TESTAR » cover « Zélvine Testar » (T30, the command line and the desktop single document); a group not joined in the command line does the same. v1.15 (the orchestrator's rulings 1 and 2): an added word is covered only when the written word and the typed one are Q3 forms of each other, either way round (typed « TESTAR » covers « Tèstar », G17; « Mèzor » is never covered for « Mézor », T31), any whitespace between the words, a line break included (T35); elsewhere the old span, as on `main`. v1.17 (LEAK-1019-02): a closing quotation mark (« ' », « ’ ») at the end of a word is not part of it, and « " », « “ », « ” », « « » and « » » end a word, so a name in quotation marks of any kind is covered; an apostrophe inside a word is an elision and stays in it (« D'Émerine »; T37 ×5). v1.19 (LEAK-1019-03; the orchestrator's instruction 1): a word is defined by what it is: letters and accent marks, with a hyphen or an apostrophe between two letters; anything else ends it, an ellipsis, a dash, a slash, an asterisk, a digit, a bracket, any quotation mark, a comma or a full stop (« Testar… », « Testar— », « Testar– », « Testar/Quovane », « Testar* », « Testar1 »: T40 ×7). v1.21 (LEAK-1019-04; the orchestrator's instruction 1): the correction is read with the document's word rule and compared word by word; what lies between two words separates them on both sides and is what the correction has there (whitespace of any kind read as one space; the full stop after an initial, a word of one letter, read with the initial; a hyphen right after an initial ends it): « ZÉLVINE J. TESTAR », « J.-P. », « J.-M. », « TESTAR, ZÉLVINE » are covered whole, the initials typed with or without their full stops (T42 ×7); a comma or a sentence's full stop the correction does not hold is never covered, nor a number (G26, G27). ## Tasks / Subtasks

> **Order is mandatory.** Task 0 checks the starting state, the answers and the merge order. Task 1 writes the tests and proves every failure on the unfixed code, on every CI leg (AC11). Task 2 implements the fix. Task 3 removes the markers and runs G6 locally. Task 4 re-checks the consumer trace. Task 5 is the CHANGELOG and the CLI reference. Task 6 is CI, the PR marked Ready for Review, and the orchestrator's read-only Codex review of the PR's final head. Task 7 is QA, before any merge. The dev never merges. **The dev agent is the BMAD dev agent (Claude)**: it writes the tests first, then the fix (AC6, AC9). No other agent writes code on this branch, and no `codex` session that writes runs on it: Codex only reviews, read-only, before the merge command (Task 6.3). If Codex cannot run, stop and tell Lionel; no review is reported that did not run.

## Dev Notes

### Decisions Recorded

Settled; do not re-open.

- **As the epic records them**, word for word from its 10.19 section at `318def4` (the rebased `7ab459e`, the same text):

1. **Q1, one person, two pseudonyms, and the erasure: "B: Erasure only, in 10.19 (Rec.)".** The question, word for word, as the PO measured and rewrote it: "10.19 Q1: the app keeps one pseudonym per exact spelling of a name, capitals included, so « Zorbal » and « ZORBAL » are two different names to it. Measured with the real detector: in « Entretien avec Quentrel Zorbal. Quentrel ZORBAL explique son parcours. », the review shows one name and you accept it once, but two rows are stored and the output reads « Entretien avec Évangéline Hamel. Évangéline Caron explique son parcours. »: one person, two pseudonyms. The same happens when the detector found « Zorbal » and you add « ZORBAL » by hand. Erasure: « delete-mapping Zorbal » removes only the « Zorbal » row and says its pseudonym is now permanently anonymous, while the « ZORBAL » row and its pseudonym stay; the search step of the documented erasure workflow (« list-entities --search Zorbal ») does list both rows. The two pseudonyms and the partial erasure are already so in v2.2.0; neither leaves anything in clear in the written files. (A) Later, all of it, as its own story after 2.3.0. (B) Before 2.3.0, in 10.19, the erasure only (Rec.): « delete-mapping » with a name also removes the rows whose name differs from it only in capitals, listed with the others before you confirm; one pseudonym per spelling stays for a later story, because changing it changes how every stored name is looked up and existing databases may already hold both spellings. (C) Before 2.3.0, in 10.19, all of it: names that differ only in capitals share one pseudonym and one row. Which one?" The options: "A: Later, all of it" / "B: Erasure only, in 10.19 (Rec.)" / "C: All of it, in 10.19". → AC4, AC6, AC7; one pseudonym per spelling → Follow-ups.
2. **The rule's wording, the PO's M1, accepted by the orchestrator without a question:** "the same letters as the text selected, whatever their capitals, except a place written entirely in small letters (unless it is exactly the text selected)", the faithful reading of Lionel's "forms with capitals only … never a word in small letters"; 0 extra places on real text. → AC1.
3. **Q2, the `delete-mapping` texts: "Approve (Rec.)".** On the first version, which said "row"/"ligne", Lionel answered: ""ligne" is confusing. Why refer to this in terms of database?" The reworded question, word for word: ""Fair point: "row"/"ligne" is how the database stores it, not how you think about it. I copied it from the current reference (line 470 says "one row each"). Same texts, reworded without database words. Screen (the number is the count): « You are about to delete the following 2 saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type or its capitals): ». CLI-REFERENCE.md: « The same name can have several saved pseudonyms: one for each type it was saved under, and one for each way it was written with capitals ("Marie Dupont", "MARIE DUPONT"). Deleting by name lists all of them, then deletes them all (each deletion is logged as an ERASURE entry); `--id` deletes only one and tells you which others remain for the same name written exactly the same way. ». CLI-REFERENCE.fr.md: « Un même nom peut avoir plusieurs pseudonymes enregistrés : un pour chaque type sous lequel il a été enregistré, et un pour chaque façon dont il a été écrit avec des majuscules (« Marie Dupont », « MARIE DUPONT »). La suppression par nom les affiche tous, puis les supprime tous (chaque suppression est consignée par une entrée ERASURE) ; `--id` n'en supprime qu'un et indique ceux qui restent pour le même nom écrit exactement de la même façon. ». Approve these?"" → AC8.
4. **Q3, accents dropped in capitals: "A: Reach it too (Rec.)".** The question, word for word: "10.19 Q3: a name written in capitals can lose its accents (« HELENE » for « Hélène »). Your capitals rule reaches « HÉLÈNE » but not « HELENE », whose letters differ. Measured on the real command line with the fix: adding « Hélène » writes « HÉLÈNE » with its pseudonym and leaves « HELENE » in clear; v2.2.0 and today's code leave both in clear. Reaching it adds no wrong replacement on the text measured: none of the app's 728 names appears in capitals, with or without accents, in your ebook (20,000 words) or in the 25 test documents. How often real documents drop accents in capitals is not measured. (A) Reach it too (Rec.): a place written entirely in capitals is also a form when its letters are the name's once accents are set aside (« HELENE », « HELÈNE »), and « delete-mapping Hélène » also deletes a saved pseudonym of « HELENE »; still never a word in small letters, and « Helene » (no accent, not in capitals) stays a different name. The desktop app (10.17) takes the same rule. (B) Keep the rule as it is: « HELENE » stays in clear, and « delete-mapping Hélène » leaves the saved pseudonym of « HELENE » (measured). Which one?" The options: "A: Reach it too (Rec.)" / "B: Keep the rule". → AC1, AC4, AC5, AC6; the desktop rule is in 10.17.
5. **Q4, corrections in desktop batch review: "A: Reach it too (Rec.)".** The question, word for word: "10.19 Q4 (desktop batch review, where each place of a name is its own row): the detector finds « Zorbal » in « Zorbal Quentrix a signé. » and « ZORBAL » in « Signé : ZORBAL QUENTRIX. ». You correct the « Zorbal » row to « Zorbal Quentrix ». Every other row written « Zorbal » takes the correction (your 10.10 rule), but the « ZORBAL » row does not: it stays to review, and if you accept it as it is, the file reads « … Signé : [pseudonym] QUENTRIX. » (measured, today and with this story's fix). If you correct that row too, nothing stays in clear. The command line and the one-document review reach « ZORBAL QUENTRIX » with this story. (A) Reach it too (Rec.): in batch review, a correction also reaches the rows of the same name and type written with other capitals (and without accents in capitals, as you just chose), except a row you rejected on its own, and never a row written entirely in small letters. (B) Keep it: each spelling is its own row, and you correct « ZORBAL » yourself. Which one?" The options: "A: Reach it too (Rec.)" / "B: Keep it". The orchestrator added "(and without accents in capitals, as you just chose)" to keep it consistent with Q3. Placement in this story: the PM (Ordering). → AC3, AC6.

- **The capitals question, word for word from the orchestrator's decision log** (`/home/lionel/.claude/MEMORY/WORK/rgpd-accuracy-bmad/stopA-answers.md`), "Capitals in "every place"":

- "Capitals in 'every place' (10.15 command line, 10.17 desktop): a name you add by hand is replaced only where it is written with exactly the capitals you typed. Measured with the real detector: in « Monsieur Zorbal a présenté le budget. Brimel Testar a validé la proposition de ZORBAL. », adding « Zorbal » leaves « ZORBAL » in clear, in a text file and in Excel. (A) Fix before 2.3.0, forms with capitals only (Rec.): « Zorbal » also reaches « ZORBAL », never a word in small letters; none of the app's 728 known names appears in capitals in your ebook (20,000 words) or in the 25 test documents, so this adds no wrong replacement there. (C) Before 2.3.0, in 10.19, all of it: names that differ only in capitals share one pseudonym and one row. Which one?" — Options: "A: Later, all of it" / "B: Erasure only, in 10.19 (Rec.)" / "C: All of it, in 10.19". — **Lionel: "B: Erasure only, in 10.19 (Rec.)"**.

New user texts say "saved pseudonym", never "row" or "ligne" (Q2); the five `delete-mapping` lines that say "mapping" stay as they are (Q5), and the `--id` line says "mapping" in place of "row" (Q5b).
- **Lionel's Q5 and Q5b**, word for word from the decision log, "Story 10.19 Q5" and "Story 10.19 Q5b, the "rows" line":

- Question (verbatim as asked): "10.19 Q5: on the same « delete-mapping » screens as your new « saved pseudonyms » line, five other lines still use database words (measured with this story's fix; they exist in English only). Current → proposed: (1) « You are about to delete the following entity mapping: » → « You are about to delete the following saved pseudonym: »; (2) « This will permanently delete the mapping. The pseudonym 'X' will become permanently anonymous. This cannot be undone. » → « This will permanently delete this saved pseudonym. … »; (3) « This will permanently delete these 2 mappings. … » → « … these 2 saved pseudonyms. … »; (4) « Entity mapping deleted successfully. » and « 2 entity mappings deleted successfully. » → « Saved pseudonym deleted successfully. » and « 2 saved pseudonyms deleted successfully. »; (5) after « --id »: « 2 other rows with the same name remain (types: LOCATION, ORG). Delete one with 'gdpr-pseudo delete-mapping --id <uuid>', or every row of the name with 'gdpr-pseudo delete-mapping "<name>"'. » → « 2 other saved pseudonyms remain for the same name written exactly the same way … ». Names and numbers follow what is deleted; the command's name « delete-mapping » stays. Reword them?" — Options: "A: Reword them (Rec.)" / "B: Keep them". — **Lionel: "Mapping is clear enough imo"** → lines with "mapping" stay as they are.

- Lionel asked first: "Rows are the same thing as mappings ?" → answered: yes, one mapping is one stored row (data/models.py). Then: "I mean, as a concept, are they the same thing?" → answered: no; "mapping" is what it means (this name, under this type, is replaced by this pseudonym), "row" is how it is stored (one entry of the storage table); in this tool they always come one for one.
- Question (verbatim as asked): "So should the line after « delete-mapping --id » use the word for what's being deleted? « 2 other mappings with the same name remain (types: LOCATION, ORG). Delete one with 'gdpr-pseudo delete-mapping --id <uuid>', or every mapping of the name with 'gdpr-pseudo delete-mapping "<name>"'. »" — Options: "Say mapping (Rec.)" (only "row"/"rows" become "mapping"/"mappings"; with one left: « 1 other mapping with the same name remains (type: ORG). ») / "Keep "rows"". — **Lionel: "Say mapping (Rec.)"**

Today it keeps 'Vorlène' and 'VORLENE' as two names, and a correction leaves 'QUENTRIX'; the desktop already reaches it. This is covered by Lionel's capitals decision for corrections plus his Q3 and Q4 answers, so no new question to him." "PO S1: use 10.17's accent-folding method (same function or the same steps), so the two stories agree on letters like Hangul." "PO N4: align the 10.7 CHANGELOG line "(one per type)" with the "saved pseudonyms" wording, and say it points to 10.19."
- **The orchestrator's ruling on the PO's v1.2 M1 (2026-10-19; the orchestrator's, not Lionel's; it applies Lionel's Q3). Its words, from its instruction to the SM:** "PO M1 (new), my ruling: keep them apart, no question to Lionel. With three spellings in one document (« Vorlène Quentrix a signé. Signé : VORLENE QUENTRIX. Vu par Vorlene Quentrix. »), today's grouping already puts « VORLENE » with « Vorlene » (same small letters), so v1.2's join and the single-document reach chain through « VORLENE » and catch « Vorlene », which Lionel's Q3 says is a different name. Measured on your prototype: the CLI writes « … Vu par Ekko Colin Quentrix. » with « Quentrix » in clear and nothing left to correct; the desktop single document does the same. Ruling: Q3 already decides it (« Vorlene » is a different name), and the other choice leaves a name in clear". **What it decides:** the CLI's join is not transitive (a group joins another of the same type only when every text of one is a form of a text of the other, each group with its own texts, never texts gathered by earlier joins), and in the desktop single document `_action_rows` reaches another row only when the text of every place of that row (`_variant_occurrences`) is a form of the corrected name. A row with exactly the same name and type is reached as today (10.10). (The orchestrator on the PO's v1.3 M1, refining this ruling: the every-place check applies only to rows reached through the forms rule, when `_name_key(other) != name`; 10.10's rule already decides the rest and the fix keeps today's behaviour; no question to Lionel.) **In rare three-spelling cases « VORLENE » stays with « Vorlene » as its own name, as today: the user sees it in the review and corrects it.** G8 pins it. Also from the same instruction: "development starts only after 10.17 has merged to main (10.19 uses 10.17's accent steps and takes over its forms functions)" (the PO's v1.2 M2), the hand-over of 10.17's helpers (S1) and the docstrings (N1).
- **The orchestrator's ruling on the second read-only Codex review, of `f8c28a6` (2026-10-26; `rgpd-analysis/r1019/codex/ruling2.md`; the findings verbatim in `review2_final.txt`, session 01a17614-f172-7950-a225-377766ed7768, VERDICT: CHANGES REQUESTED, the first two P0 confirmed fixed), its words:** "All three are inside Lionel's scope: French names, capitals, and Q3's own example « HELÈNE ». All three break rules already set, so they are fixed in this story with no question for Lionel." 1: "a later correction of the same name reaches every row the earlier correction reached, through the text now written at that row (the widened span). Applies to single-document review and batch review." 2: "in batch review, a row the forms rule includes is modified only if the corrected text reaches it, read in the text written at that row, in Q3's direction. Otherwise the row stays pending, as on main. Nothing is ever written with a partial span." 3: "when comparing Q3 forms of 10.20's saved forms, every accent mark counts as "a mark", whatever its kind. The sign rule still holds: a lost-letter form only matches a row that still carries some mark." The class checks widened, every miss reported (Reproduction Evidence v1.13). The orchestrator confirmed rule 2 in single-document review too, where a row stands for several places, its ruling: "Nothing is ever written with a partial span", single and batch (T29; the class check found the same partial spans there at `f8c28a6`).
- **The orchestrator's ruling for v1.27 (2026-10-30), its words:** the ninth read-only Codex review (session 01a18cbb, `rgpd-analysis/r1019/codex/review9_final.txt`): « ́LANIE (́LISSA) TESTAR », the old steps' row of a parenthesised given name, missed by the name typed without accents, "`bound_before` rejects the cut after `(`, although the old title pattern allows it". "A parenthesised second given name is realistic, so this gets fixed in 10.19 (Lionel's stop rule). Fix the class, not the case." "Define `bound_before` from the old title pattern's own bound: read it from v2.2.0's regex, and allow exactly what that regex allows before a title. The walk only offers candidates and the old step's replay decides, so offering what the old step allows is safe by construction. Apply the same bound to the inserted-word skip." Not a regression: `63da306`, `6d87d22`, `c2379c4` and `5d110a4` miss the same spellings, and `main` reaches neither row unless the name is typed in its saved capitals. QA's re-check of `5d110a4` (`73bbb76`, gate CONCERNS 90): DEL-1019-10 (medium) is the review's P0 on real v2.2.0 rows; its shapes « MÉLORA (MÉLIME) TESTAR » and « QUOVANE (MÉLORA) » open the proof set, the second missed even typed with accents: its old row « QUOVANE (́LORA) » has no word starting with a mark, so 10.20's replay drops it as a cut without a sign. The class fix reads that sign with the same bound: a mark right after a character the pattern lets a title word follow, « ( » as much as a space (T51). The place's « A » at the row's end, which `40588e4` offers and no test covers: the old steps cannot make a row that needs it, since 10.20's replay puts « à » in front of a place only (`_key_texts`, `core/mapping_key.py:144`), and the punctuated and wide sets find the same rows with it and without it; Task 17 removes it. OBS-1019-06 and « Quovomaitre Testar »: Backlog. A consequence, measured: a mark after « - » or « ' » is the same sign, so 10.20's gap narrows (QA's two-word hyphen probe `test_q19_r3c` passes 13 of 13, 9 since v1.15; `class_delete_typed2` and `_typed3` 84 → 0 rows of the gap), with 0 touched on every set; what stays in the gap is a cut that leaves no lone mark.
- **The orchestrator's rule for v1.25 (2026-10-30), its words:** the eighth read-only Codex review (session 01a18c32, `rgpd-analysis/r1019/codex/review8_final.txt`) found « QUOVO » + U+0302 + « MAI » + U+0302 + « TRE TESTAR », the old steps' row of decomposed « QUOVÔME MAÎTRE TESTAR », missed by « Quovome Testar », "a REGRESSION: 6d87d22 found the row". "Passing over 10.20's inserted words (« MAITRE », a place's leading « A ») must be allowed wherever the walk already allows a title cut": the row's start, after a separator, a space or any mark, a mark a cut left alone included. "Use one predicate for both moves, so the two rules can't drift apart again." The proof's wide generator now puts « Maître » with and without its accent at every position, glued after cuts, and a place's leading « à ». Found measuring: a « MAÎTRE » kept after the name's last word (« ́LORA VORLAN MAÎTRE ») was found by `6d87d22` and missed since `63da306`; fixed as a regression too (T48): at the row's end, the space before the inserted word is its bound. A third, found on the wide set with QA's examples added: a row of the name's capitals with every accent set aside that lost a whole title word (« ELORIA-MELORA MAÎTRE VORLAN » for « Éloria-Mèlora Mmé Vorlan »), found by `6d87d22`, lost since `63da306`: the walk's empty set (no letter accented) is now replayed too (T49). The orchestrator accepted T48 under the regression ruling. QA's re-check of `c2379c4` (`eafe8c6`, gate CONCERNS 90): DEL-1019-09 (medium) is the review's P0, reproduced on real v2.2.0 rows (64 rows lost against `63da306` and 4,506 against `6d87d22` on QA's wide run, each with « MAITRE » glued after a cut or after the name); its examples « QUOVÔME MAÎTRE TESTAR », « BÉLORA MÉLORA MAÎTRE » and « ÉLORIA MÈLORA MAÎTRE » open the proof set. TEST-1019-08 (low): no committed test read a « Ç », so the mutant without « C » survived; G30 (« MÉLORA VORÇANE ») is that guard.
- **The orchestrator's ruling for v1.23 (2026-10-30), its words:** the seventh read-only Codex review (session 01a18bbc, `rgpd-analysis/r1019/codex/review7_final.txt`) found « ̀TESTAR », v2.2.0's row of « MÈME TESTAR », missed by « Meme Testar », "a REGRESSION from 63da306"; "Under Lionel's rule a regression gets fixed, whatever its realism. A mark that a previous cut left detached counts as a place where a cut can still happen. The walk must accept any sequence of cuts the old title step could make", the old step's own logic deciding, "not hand-coded branches". "Keep the sign rule and the capitals-only boundary." The proof is extended with every shape the old step makes (cuts before and after one mark, nested cuts, runs of title words in one word). DEL-1019-08: QA calls it exotic, Codex calls it plausible, and the ruling is that it is fixed because it is a regression; QA's note that `46202a3` fixes « MÉMÉ TESTAR », which `63da306` missed, is kept (G29). The proof script runs on any run's outputs, as QA's `exact_qa7.py` does. ### Ordering and Rebase

The epic's rule, word for word from its Execution Sequence at `318def4` (`main`'s block as 10.20 left it, `e93c55e`, with 10.19's line from `3fe0d59`):

**Stories 10.12 to 10.18, and 10.20,** are release blockers outside the chain: none changes detection code, so none has an accuracy baseline (10.20 makes one accuracy run as a check, its AC7). Each is its own PR, never shared with another story, and all eight land before the v2.3.0 release. Stories that change the same file are sequenced, and the later one is rebased on the earlier; they are never merged together. Most shared files are different functions, with two exceptions: 10.18 needs 10.15's code (its [A], the cells it gives the manual flow, and its F1 fix) and both change `_run_validation`; and 10.13, 10.15, 10.16 and 10.18 all edit the same `run_validation_workflow(…)` call (`core/document_processor.py:363-372`, its keyword arguments at `:368-370`), so every keyword argument is kept at each rebase. 10.20 goes after 10.16 and before 10.17 and 10.19; its shared files are in its Ordering. The shared files:
- 10.13, 10.15 and 10.18: `validation/workflow.py` and `validation/ui.py` (10.13 and 10.15 also `validation/models.py`); 10.18 reuses [A] as 10.15 leaves it.
- 10.13, 10.16 and 10.17 (and 10.14 if it changes it): `gui/models/validation_state.py`.
- 10.13 and 10.14: `gui/screens/validation.py` (`_on_finalize`'s batch branch) and the `gui/i18n/` files. The `.qm` files are binary: they are regenerated after a rebase, never merged.
- 10.15 and 10.18: `_run_validation` in `core/document_processor.py` (the same function). 10.16 shares the file and the `run_validation_workflow(…)` call's keyword arguments (`:368-370`), not the skip; its own points, as its SM measured them, are `:172-178`, `:368-370`, `:392-414`, `:416-482`, `:635-652` and `:737-739`. It does not change `:801` (Codex #5's save, in the backlog). 10.15 and 10.16 may also both change `validation/workflow.py`.
- 10.19 (added 2026-10-19): `validation/models.py` with 10.13 and 10.15; `validation/workflow.py` with 10.13, 10.15 and 10.18; the `delete-mapping` code (`cli/commands/delete_mapping.py`, `data/repositories/mapping_repository.py`) with 10.12, now merged; the callers of `_locate_modified_span` in `gui/models/validation_state.py` with 10.17. It starts after 10.15 merges.
- Every story edits `CHANGELOG.md`; 10.12 shares no other file.

Suggested order: 10.12 at any time; 10.13, then 10.14, then 10.16, then 10.17 on the desktop side; 10.15 after 10.13, then 10.18 on the CLI side (every question is answered). 10.16 has no question and can go before 10.14 if needed.

**For 10.19 (SM):**
- **Merged since `90a1646`:** 10.18 (`ff678fc`, PR #107), 10.14 (`43041e9`, PR #108), 10.16 (`3114e9e`, PR #109), 10.20 (`e93c55e`, PR #111) and 10.17 (`097a12c`, PR #110). The branch was rebased on `097a12c` on 2026-10-25 (Branch); Task 0.2 holds. 10.19 has no Epic 10 story left to wait for.
- **10.15** (`90a1646`): its `places_of`, `is_listed_place`, `_widen_added_places` (with `TYPED_SOURCES`) and the approved line are the base this story changes.
- **10.18** (merged): its question when nothing is left to review (`ask_for_missed_names`, `workflow.py:720-772`) calls the manual flow and its [A], so it reaches the forms too (Consumer Trace, row 8); no 10.18 test types a name whose document holds another spelling of it, so none changes (measured: the existing suites pass on the prototype).
- **10.17** (merged; development starts only after it, the PO's v1.2 M2, and it has): its forms helpers in `gui/models/validation_state.py` (`fold`, `is_form`, `whole_word_places`, `:65-136`) are the one rule for both apps. **One rule for both apps, at Task 2.8 (the PO's v1.2 S1):** they move unchanged to `validation/models.py` (no `gui/` import there) and `validation_state.py` imports back the two it uses; no other module and no test imports them from `validation_state.py` (Code Map), and 10.17's tests stay unchanged and green (measured on the SM's prototype v1.5). 10.17's every-place rows: a row added by hand stands for all its places in single-document review (`_occurrences`, which `_action_rows` reads, Fix Design AC3) and is one row per place in batch (`rows_per_place`). G9 adds a row by hand through 10.17's add (Task 1.3 records it).
- **10.20** (merged): composed readers and typed text, combining marks inside words, every saved form of a name found, the composed name in `delete-mapping`, and Lionel's Qc line on its screen. 10.19 adds no normalization of its own (Fix Design, AC1 item 3; T19); its any-capitals erasure keeps 10.20's rows for the name and compares the others composed (Fix Design, AC4); its Q2 line replaces Qc's, and 10.20's test of that line changes with it (Task 3.2).
- **10.16** (merged): custom pseudonyms (`_pseudonym_rows`, the review's names); 10.19 does not change them.
- **10.12 (merged):** it owns `delete-mapping`'s ERASURE entries (one per row, no name). 10.19 changes only which saved pseudonyms the name finds (`:135`, `:165`) and the several-row line (`:309-315`, Q2); the entry loop (`:178-201`) does not change.
- **The rule:** never merged together. Whichever merges second rebases on `main` with the first merged, re-runs its tests-first proof on the rebased tests-first commit (the same xfailed count on CI), gets CI green on the rebased head, has the read-only Codex review run on the rebased head (Task 6.3), and QA re-checks the rebased diff before the merge command. `CHANGELOG.md`: keep every story's entry, in merge order.

### Gates G1–G7 (verbatim from the epic)

The following block is normative and is referenced by every story's acceptance criteria.

- **G1 — Metric evidence.** Every reported metric comes from a CI accuracy run on the story branch: `gh workflow run accuracy.yaml --ref <branch> -f reason="<story id>"`. Cite the run ID. Paste the `[Overall]`, `[PERSON]`, `[LOCATION]`, `[ORG]` lines from the `accuracy-results` artifact (`accuracy-output.txt`). Numbers typed by the dev agent without a run ID do not count.
- **G2 — QA verifies independently.** The QA agent downloads the cited run's artifact itself (`gh run download <id> -n accuracy-results`) and checks the numbers match the story's claims. QA never accepts the dev's text as evidence.
- **G3 — Recall guard.** Overall recall and each per-type recall must not drop versus the previous merged baseline. Any drop: stop and ask Lionel.
- **G4 — Attribution.** Changes to annotations/scorer and changes to detection code are never in the same PR. Benchmark-driven deltas and detector-driven deltas are reported separately.
- **G5 — Held-out check.** Once the held-out set exists (story 10.1), every detector story also reports held-out P/R/F1. A gain on the main corpus with a loss on held-out is a red flag for overfitting: report it.
- **G6 — Normal quality.** black, ruff, mypy clean; unit tests for every new rule; full CI green (one re-run allowed for the known macOS flake).
- **G7 — Docs.** If a story changes the headline numbers, it updates README/README.fr, `docs/faq*.md`, `docs/index*.md`, `docs/tutorial*.md`, the QA report and CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]`, all from the G1 run.

#### Epic 10 G7 interpretation (Lionel, 2026-10-02)

Decided by Lionel, 2026-10-02. This note interprets the gates for Epic 10; the gate text above is unchanged.

- **G7 — per story vs. close-out.** The QA report (`docs/qa/ner-accuracy-report.md`) records the before/after at every story (10.1 marked "benchmark repair, same detector"), and each story adds its CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` entry. README/README.fr, `docs/faq*.md`, `docs/index*.md` and `docs/tutorial*.md` change once, at Epic 10 close-out, from the final G1 run — not per story.
- **G3 under a ground-truth change (10.1).** G3 does not block 10.1. 10.1's close-out run becomes the new baseline. Every per-type recall drop between the old and new ground truth is listed with its cause (e.g. "+N ORG annotations added") in the story and the PR. G3 applies strictly from 10.2 onwards.

**How they apply to 10.19 (AC10):**
- **G1, G3 and G5: N/A.** No detection change, and no file the accuracy suite loads is touched (AC precisions, AC10; Task 6.1). The PR says so. *Conditional clause:* if any such file is touched, one regular `accuracy.yaml` run on the branch shows the 16 lines identical to `38212303870`, cited by ID.
- **G2:** QA re-runs the new tests, runs them on the tests-first commit with `--runxfail`, re-runs the PO's capitals probes, reads the diff and checks end to end (Task 7), before the merge.
- **G4:** never in the same PR as a detector change, or as another Epic 10 story.
- **G6:** in full. Reruns are for infrastructure failures only, one per CI run.
- **G7:** the CHANGELOG entry and the CLI reference sentences (AC7, AC8); no headline number changes.
- **No skip marker anywhere** (Task 6.4).

### Product Constraints (non-negotiable, verbatim from the epic)

These apply to every story in this epic. A story that cannot meet its goal inside them stops and goes back to Lionel; it does not relax them.

1. **Local NLP only.** No LLM, no generative model, no API call, no cloud service, no telemetry. Every change is a deterministic rule, lexicon or pattern that runs offline.
2. **No encoder NER models.** Introducing a transformer/encoder NER model (e.g. CamemBERT-NER) is a Lionel decision that belongs to Epic 9. It must not be introduced in this epic, including as an optional dependency or experiment committed to the repo.
3. **Mandatory human validation stays.** No auto-accept mode, no `--no-validate`, no confidence-based skipping. Precision gains reduce the reviewer's workload; they do not replace the reviewer.
4. **No user data** for training, tuning or evaluation. All corpus and held-out documents are synthetic.
5. **Recall over precision.** Missing a real person is worse than showing an extra candidate. Any drop in overall recall or in any per-type recall needs Lionel's sign-off (gate G3).
6. **No public accuracy claim without CI evidence.** Every number that reaches README, FAQ, docs, CHANGELOG or the QA report comes from a cited CI accuracy run (gate G1).

### Cross-Story Rules (verbatim from the epic)

These notes sit outside the normative Gates block above and do not change its text. They bind every detector story from 10.3a on: 10.3a, 10.3b, 10.3c and 10.4 (10.3c added, orchestrator 2026-10-07; rule text unchanged).

- **Coverage level 2b (Lionel, 2026-10-06):** No rule may remove the last covering span of any type from an annotation of any type without Lionel's sign-off.

For 10.19: the rule binds detector stories. 10.19 changes no detection, so the rule is met trivially. AC10 and Task 6.1 are the guard.

### Isolation Rules

These apply to the dev agent (the BMAD dev agent, Claude), the orchestrator, the QA agent and the PO.

The blocks below are copied word for word from the 10.11 story at `318def4` (unchanged since `7ab459e`) (Dev Notes "Isolation Rules"), which copies them from the 10.10, 10.8, 10.6 and 10.4 stories. The task and AC references inside are those stories'; "How they bind 10.19" maps them to this story.

**Standing rules, word for word from the 10.6 story** (Dev Notes "Isolation Rules", "Standing rules (word for word, as given for every story from 10.6 on …)", at `main` `4f3cf30`):

- **Held-out isolation:** no agent opens, lists, reads or greps anything under `tests/test_corpus/held_out/`. Held-out results are used only as aggregate lines in a CI `accuracy-results` artifact. Any `tests/unit/held_out_leakage_allowlist.json` change needs Lionel's recorded approval.
- **Final-exam isolation, from the start of 10.6 authoring:** no agent opens, lists or greps the final-exam material (isolated workspace or `tests/test_corpus/final_exam/`). Orchestrating agents handle only paths, hashes and counts. Final-exam numbers are never used for a decision or for tuning.
- **Held-out-blind strings:** any real string in a new test or resource must occur in the main-corpus text or be Lionel-approved. Use invented names: Zorbalia, Quentrix, Zorbaville, Quentrel ("Quentin" is real).
- **CI must be all green to merge, and a red check is fixed inside the current PR.** Reruns are for infrastructure failures only. Absolute wall-clock test asserts are banned.
- **Recall over precision, and the coverage invariant:** not applicable to PR A, but restated so later stories keep them (see "Product Constraints and Cross-Story Rules" below).

- The leakage guard `tests/unit/test_held_out_leakage.py` (non-slow, runs in normal CI) fails if a held-out-only entity string appears in `gdpr_pseudonymizer/resources/**` or `tests/unit/**` (`SEARCH_TREES`, `:34`). This story adds files to both trees (new resources, new tests, edited `detection_patterns.yaml`). The guard is the only check that your strings and list entries are not held-out strings.
- **Allowlist:** `tests/unit/held_out_leakage_allowlist.json` is keyed by (path, sha256). Any change to it needs **Lionel's recorded approval in this story** (date + what). QA flags any PR diff on it.
- **If the leakage guard fires:** the message gives a path and a hash only. Do not try to find out which string it is.
  - **Unit test file with invented strings only:** replace your synthetic string with another clearly invented one.
  - **Unit test file that contains AC-named or other real strings:** treat it like a resource. Do **not** bisect, edit or remove strings to find the hit. HALT and send the whole file (path, hash, list of its real strings) to Lionel.
  - **A resource file** (stoplist, role words, particles, AC3 lexicon, `detection_patterns.yaml`): do **not** remove, edit or bisect entries, because each removal would reveal which entry is a held-out string. HALT and send the **whole frozen list** (or the lexicon reference) to Lionel with the path and hash. Lionel either allowlists it (path + hash + date recorded here and in the allowlist) or drops the category concerned. Record his decision in Decisions Recorded.
  - Either way, note it in Completion Notes by hash only.

- **Calibration (Lionel, 2026-10-09):** the guards target real channels: text actually in a prompt or a file, public CI logs, and the held-out and final-exam access rules. No guard assumes that a model session remembers anything across calls; each GPT or Gemini call starts from fresh context.

**How they bind 10.19:**
- The search scope is Task 0.4; the string check is Task 1.5; any allowlist change needs Lionel's recorded approval in this story.
- 10.19 adds two test files to `tests/unit/` and no resource; both leakage guards scan them on every CI run.
- The final exam stays closed; the dev agent's brief names no held-out or final-exam path.
- The new files hold invented names only (AC14), main-corpus words (pre-checked, Test Plan), an invented passphrase (`zorbalia_pass_A_123!`), an invented sheet name, file names and code identifiers. The real word « Hélène » of AC5 appears only in the SM's measurement files, never in a committed test (T10 and T13 use the invented « Vorlène »).
- **The user's settings file is never touched:** every test points `HOME` and `USERPROFILE` at its `tmp_path`, runs there, and points `gui.config._config_path` into it; every `.bat` runner sets `USERPROFILE` and `HOME` to a scratch folder. Never touch `C:\Users\devea\.gdpr-pseudo.yaml`.
- **No absolute wall-clock assert**, no sleep; the desktop test waits on a condition with a generous timeout that only stops a hang.
